# Decision Log

Append one row for every decision the docs don't cover (standing rule 2, [build-prompts-v1.md](build-prompts-v1.md)). Doc conflicts are escalated to the human, never resolved by editing `docs/`.

| Date | Decision | Alternatives considered | Reason | Prompted by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-07-24 | DOM-based canvas: CSS-transformed stage hosting one live `<iframe>` per route | Raw Canvas API rendering of page previews | Frames run the real Vite preview build, so what the user sees is the actual code (preview = handover); selection chrome renders in a parent-document overlay; Canvas API would mean re-implementing rendering and losing the live build | canvas-editor-prd-v1.md §2.1 |
| 2026-07-24 | Override layer: edits stored as patches, applied at render time, compiled into source only at export | Patching source files live on every canvas gesture (bidirectional code editing) | Editing stays instant and generation-independent — no code files change during editing; overrides survive section regeneration and compile deterministically at export | codegen-contract-v1.md §6 |
| 2026-07-24 | Kitaru runtime over hand-rolled orchestration | Custom checkpoint/replay/resume implementation | Checkpoints, crash resume, and replay-with-overrides come stock; replay-with-overrides directly powers section regeneration — the regen fork is the only hand-rolled runtime work | agent-pipeline-spec-v1.md §5 |
| 2026-07-24 | No reviewer / critic / reconciliation agents in v1 | LLM review pass between generation and assembly | Consistency is enforced by constraint (ownership rules, tokens, mechanical gates), which is the token-conservative architecture; a review-pass slot stays reserved between stages 5 and 6 if quality data ever justifies it | agent-pipeline-spec-v1.md §2.6 |
| 2026-07-24 | Sections generate sequentially within a page; pages fan out in parallel | Fully parallel sections; fully sequential whole-site generation | Each section prompt includes summaries of prior sections on its page, so the page reads coherently (hero and CTA can't contradict); cross-page consistency is carried entirely by tokens, primitives, and shell, so pages need no coordination | agent-pipeline-spec-v1.md §2.5 |
| 2026-07-24 | Root check command is `npm run check` (no Makefile) | `make check` | Development happens on Windows, where `make` is not dependably available; npm workspaces already orchestrate compiler/editor and `uv run --directory orchestrator pytest` covers Python; CI runs the identical command | P0 scaffold session (build-plan allowed either) |
| 2026-07-24 | CSS custom property naming: JSON path segments joined with `-`, key names verbatim (camelCase preserved) — e.g. `--color-semantic-textMuted`, `--typography-fontFamily-heading` | Kebab-casing all keys | Matches the contract's own examples (`--color-semantic-accent`, `--space-4`) and is trivially deterministic for the 1.2 deriver, which must reproduce the fixture's tokens.css byte-identically | 1.1 fixture (contract §3.2 gives examples, not a rule) |
| 2026-07-24 | `manifest.editable` values are the §6.1 override channel set: `text`, `style`, `layout`, `visibility` | The §5.4 example's values (`typography`, `spacing`, `color`) | §5.4's prose says editable "declares which override channels the editor may offer" and §6.1 defines exactly four channels; the example's finer values are read as illustrative facets of `style`. Escalated to human for confirmation | 1.1 fixture (internal contract ambiguity §5.4 vs §6.1) |
| 2026-07-24 | Shell elements (nav, footer) carry no node IDs; shell is not canvas-editable in v1 | Extending the ID scheme with a shell scope | The §5.2 ID format is route-scoped (`<route-slug>.<section-slug>.<element-path>`) and cannot express route-independent shell elements; §5.1's "purely structural wrappers" carve-out covers centering Containers too | 1.1 fixture |
| 2026-07-24 | Fixture styling via Tailwind 4 utilities referencing token CSS vars (`bg-(--color-semantic-accent)`, `px-(--space-6)`), never inline `style` objects on addressable nodes | Inline style objects; hand-rolled utility stylesheet | Inline styles would beat both the shim's `[data-node-id]`-keyed override stylesheet (PRD §2.2) and the exporter's className merge (contract §7.2), breaking preview=export. Contract §3.2 names the Tailwind theme mapping as the sanctioned route | 1.1 fixture |
| 2026-07-24 | Fixture is a standalone npm project, not a root workspace member | Adding `fixtures/acme-landing` to root workspaces | The fixture stands in for a generated/exported project, which is standalone by definition; hoisted workspace deps would hide resolution problems an exported zip would hit | 1.1 fixture |
| 2026-07-24 | `tailwindTheme` output shape: classic Tailwind theme object (`colors`, `spacing`, `fontSize`, ...) with `var(--...)` reference values; `screens` as literal values | Tailwind 4 `@theme` CSS block; resolved literal values everywhere | var() references keep tokens.css the single source of truth (one place to change a token); screens must be literal because media queries cannot read CSS vars; a plain object is consumable by both a Tailwind config and the 1.5 exporter's token→class resolution | 1.2 deriver (contract §3 names the mapping but not its shape) |
| 2026-07-24 | Positional-ID rejection is a blocklist of generic structural segment names with numeric suffixes (`child-3`, `item-2`, `wrapper-1`, ...), not "any `-N` suffix"; IDs allow ≥2 segments | Rejecting every numeric-suffixed segment; requiring ≥3 segments | Contract §5.2's own example blesses data-derived indexed slugs (`tier-1` from `tiers[0].name`), so numeric suffixes per se are legal; and the fixture's section root `home.hero` is a valid 2-segment ID (element-path only exists below section roots) | 1.3 manifest service |
| 2026-07-24 | Tombstoned IDs may be re-registered when file AND component match the tombstoned entry (flips back to active); only a different file/component is rejected | Rejecting all resurrection | Contract §5.4's tombstone rule forbids re-registration "with a different file/component", implying same-identity re-registration is legal — which regeneration (M4) needs when an element is removed and later restored | 1.3 manifest service |
| 2026-07-24 | Gate 1 is static: relative-import resolution + bare-import presence in package.json; the "build passes" half of contract §8.1 is enforced by the exporter's verification build (§7.4) and the M1 spine command | Running a full npm build inside the gate | Gates must be cheap code runnable on minimal broken-fixture trees with no node_modules; a real build per gate run would need installs and add ~10s+ per section retry loop | 1.4 gates |
| 2026-07-24 | Gate 2 external whitelist default: `https://`, `http://`, `mailto:`, `tel:`, and `#` anchors pass; any href starting with `/` must exist in routes.ts; only string-literal hrefs are checked (dynamic `href={expr}` values are covered at runtime by route-table-sourced data) | Explicit per-project whitelist config from day one | Contract says "whitelisted external" without defining the list; a protocol-level default unblocks v1, and a config hook can narrow it when a real need appears | 1.4 gates |
| 2026-07-24 | Gate 5 scope: JSX text nodes and string-literal expression children inside `pages/*/sections/*.tsx` only; JSX attributes and shell files are exempt | Scanning all files and all attributes | Contract §8.5 targets "section JSX"; attributes (`variant="primary"`, `href="/"`) are not user-visible copy, and the shell legitimately carries brand strings the Shell Agent bakes in | 1.4 gates |
| 2026-07-24 | Gate 6 checks cross-page imports statically always; the out-of-boundary-write check runs only when an orchestrator write log (`writtenFiles`) is supplied | Inferring writes from the file tree | "Who wrote what" is runtime knowledge a bare project tree cannot reveal; the contract's own wording ("checked via file diff") presumes a write log, which exists from M3 onward | 1.4 gates |
| 2026-07-25 | Export output contains no override files at all; archiving overrides is the editor's export-flow job (M6), acting on the source project | Archiving into the export artifact | Contract §7 says overrides are "consumed" by export; carrying archives inside the handover artifact breaks export idempotence (export-of-export would differ) and pollutes the handover zip | 1.5 exporter |
| 2026-07-25 | Gate 3 exempts hex/px inside `-[...]` arbitrary-value utility classes | Failing exports containing compiled free values | Contract tension: §6.1/§7.2 sanction the user's deliberate free-value escape compiling to arbitrary classes (`w-[480px]`), while §8.3 bans raw px. Gate 3's intent is stopping agents from bypassing tokens; user escapes are badged in the UI and listed in HANDOVER.md. Escalated to human | 1.5 exporter |
| 2026-07-25 | Unknown style property, or token-shaped ref resolving to no token, aborts the export with a named error | Silently skipping or passing values through | A failed export never ships silently degraded code (§7.4); a typo like `color.semantic.acent` compiling to an arbitrary class would silently diverge preview from handover | 1.5 exporter |
| 2026-07-25 | className merge strips same-category utilities before appending the compiled class | Naive append-last | Tailwind resolves conflicting utilities by stylesheet order, not class order — appended `bg-(--x)` would NOT reliably beat an existing `bg-(--y)`; replacement is the only deterministic "last wins" | 1.5 exporter |
| 2026-07-25 | Verification build borrows the source project's node_modules via a directory junction, removed after the build | npm install per export | An install per export adds ~10-30s and network variance to every export and test run; the junction is read-only reuse and the export artifact ships without node_modules either way | 1.5 exporter |
| 2026-07-25 | The shim is injected by the preview server (`startPreviewServer` adds the Vite plugin via inline config); projects never depend on or reference shim code | Making the shim a dependency of fixture/generated projects, stripped at build time | "Dev-mode only, stripped at export" (PRD 2.2) holds by construction — the handover artifact cannot contain shim traces because the project never knew about the shim; nothing to strip means nothing to forget to strip | 2.1 shim |
| 2026-07-25 | Shim override rules use `!important` in the injected `[data-node-id]` sheet | Relying on cascade order alone | Vite/Tailwind append style tags dynamically during dev/HMR, so "our sheet is last" is not guaranteed over time; `!important` makes preview overrides deterministic, and the 2.4 invariant suite (screenshot diff vs export) remains the fidelity enforcement | 2.1 shim |
| 2026-07-25 | Playwright e2e joins `npm run check` (and CI installs Chromium + fixture deps) | Separate e2e command outside the check | CLAUDE.md's rule is "one root command runs every package's test suite" and CI runs exactly that command; the invariant suite (2.4) must be a required CI check, so the harness wiring starts now | 2.1 shim |
| 2026-07-25 | Geometry rects are iframe-viewport coordinates; the shim re-reports on scroll | Page coordinates (scroll included) | Editor and preview are cross-origin (different ports), so the parent cannot read the frame's scroll position to convert page coords; viewport coords map 1:1 onto the frame's box and stay correct as the user scrolls inside the frame | 2.2 editor |
| 2026-07-25 | Overlay is pointer-transparent; clicks land in the iframe and selection flows through the shim's `node:hit`; the editor reclaims keyboard focus (`window.focus()`) after each hit so Esc works | Overlay intercepts pointer events and hit-tests geometry rects parent-side | Build-plan M2 specifies "selection via node:hit"; the DOM's own `closest()` is the truth for deepest-node hits, whereas rect hit-testing double-implements it and mismatches on overlapping/rotated layouts. Cross-origin focus steal is the cost, solved by refocusing on hit | 2.2 editor |
| 2026-07-25 | Editor fetches manifest.json over HTTP from the preview server (CORS enabled in `startPreviewServer`) | Bundling/duplicating the manifest into the editor | The preview server already serves the project root, so the manifest stays single-sourced with the project; when M3 swaps in generated projects nothing changes editor-side | 2.2 editor |
| 2026-07-26 | Primitive variant knowledge is an editor-side STUB (`editor/src/lib/inventory.ts`): variants + per-variant token declarations for the fixture's four primitives | Reading variants from primitive source at runtime | The editor cannot read TS union types in the browser; the real source of this data is the Design System Agent's primitive inventory (pipeline 2.3, M5) — the stub must be replaced then. Variant persists as `{ variant }` in the style value (contract 7.2 compiles it to a prop change; exporter support required before variant joins the invariant matrix); the shim receives the variant expanded into its declarations for live preview | 2.3 style channel |
| 2026-07-26 | Editor persistence endpoints live on the preview server: GET/PUT `/__overrides/<route-slug>` and `/__overrides-history` (history at `overrides/.editor-history.json`) | A separate file-API process; browser-only persistence | The preview server already owns the project directory and its lifetime matches the editing session; `overrides/` is the editor's exclusive write space (contract section 2), so the undo history lives there too ("persisted with the project", PRD 6) | 2.3 style channel |
| 2026-07-26 | Preview server watcher ignores `overrides/**` | Letting Vite watch everything | The editor writes override/history files while the preview is live; watcher reactions to those writes interrupt the very frame being edited (observed as mid-edit frame reloads in e2e). The preview app never consumes overrides/ — the shim applies them via postMessage | 2.3 style channel |
| 2026-07-26 | Editor e2e runs against a disposable fixture copy in `generated/editor-e2e-project`, prepared inside the preview webServer command | Serving the fixture directly; Playwright globalSetup | Style tests write override files, and the fixture is permanent ground truth that must stay pristine; setup lives in the webServer command because Playwright launches webServers before globalSetup runs | 2.3 style channel |
| 2026-07-26 | Invariant suite captures the edited preview from a bare preview page (persisted overrides re-applied through the shim protocol), not through the editor's iframe | Screenshotting nodes through the editor page | Element screenshots through the editor composite parent-document chrome and selection/spacing overlays into the clip (observed: 44% false diff); the bare page is pixel-symmetric with the export capture — same viewport, same shim pathway the editor uses. Edits are still driven through real editor UI | 2.4 invariant suite |
| 2026-07-26 | Invariant threshold: ≤1% differing pixels at pixelmatch threshold 0.2, equal box dimensions required; failures dump preview/export/diff PNGs to test-results | Zero-tolerance equality | Same browser and widths make real divergence enormous (the broken-merge check produces 94.8%); the 1% headroom absorbs antialiasing only. Artifacts make failures diagnosable at a glance | 2.4 invariant suite |
| 2026-07-26 | Tier→model mapping: top=`claude-sonnet-5`, mid/small=`claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` | Opus-class for top | Pipeline §3 names tiers, not models; Sonnet 5 quality at its price fits the <$10/site target (930k-token budget), and upgrading any tier later is a one-line config change | 3.1 orchestrator |
| 2026-07-26 | Anthropic SDK wrapped as a custom `@checkpoint` (`call_model`), not `kitaru.llm()` | kitaru.llm() adapter | Structured output (needed from 3.3) is "on the roadmap" for kitaru.llm(); wrapping our own call is Kitaru's documented extension path (pipeline 5.1 anticipated exactly this) and keeps prompt caching + token accounting under our control | 3.1 orchestrator |
| 2026-07-26 | Resume idiom: `flow.replay(exec_id, at=<earliest recorded checkpoint>, skip=[same])` | Anchoring without `skip` | Kitaru has no dedicated resume API; `at` alone RE-EXECUTES the anchored checkpoint (re-billing it), while `skip` plays back its recorded result — proven live: resume made no API call and the usage log gained no rows | 3.1 orchestrator |
| 2026-07-26 | Flow bodies never subscript checkpoint outputs; results pass as handles into downstream checkpoints, which materialize them | Peeking into results in the flow body | On replay, played-back outputs are lazy artifact handles (`_KitaruOutputArtifact`), and subscripting them crashes; shaping data inside checkpoints is Kitaru's own documented pattern. Load-bearing for every future flow | 3.1 orchestrator |
| 2026-07-26 | Token accounting rows are written inside the model-call step body; pytest stays offline (pure functions only) with the crash-resume proof as a scripted live check | Wrapping accounting around checkpoints; live API tests in CI | Skipped/cached checkpoints append nothing to the log, which makes "resume does not re-bill" directly assertable from row counts; CI has no API key, so live verification cannot be a pytest | 3.1 orchestrator |
| 2026-07-27 | Rendered prompts split at the cache seam: `system` = [SYSTEM]+[DESIGN CONTEXT] (one cache_control block), `user` = the per-section blocks; the SYSTEM block contains no placeholders | Caching SYSTEM only; placeholders in SYSTEM | Pipeline §6 names "SYSTEM + DESIGN CONTEXT prefix, identical across all sections of a run" as the largest caching win; any placeholder in the prefix would fragment the cache per section | 3.2 prompts |
| 2026-07-27 | DESIGN CONTEXT budget checked with a conservative chars/3.5 estimator | Anthropic count-tokens API; a tokenizer dependency | No local tokenizer exists for Claude models and the budget check must run offline in pytest; ~4 chars/token is typical for English+code, so /3.5 systematically overcounts — the <600 assertion cannot pass on an undercount | 3.2 prompts |
| 2026-07-27 | Page-agent structured output schema fixed in the template SYSTEM block: `{files, manifestProposals, sectionMeta, orphanedOverrides}` | Deferring the schema to 3.3 | The schema is part of the prompt contract and belongs to the versioned template; 3.3's parser and manifest-proposal path consume exactly this shape, and sectionMeta.summary feeds later sections' PAGE CONTEXT | 3.2 prompts |
| 2026-07-27 | Orchestrator invokes gates and the manifest service through the compiler CLIs (`gates --json`, new `manifest propose/commit`), never re-implementing them in Python | Porting the service/gates to Python | The contract's enforcement must have exactly one implementation; two would drift. CLI JSON output is the integration seam | 3.3 section pipeline |
| 2026-07-27 | Every checkpointed step takes the attempt number in its args | Same args across retries | Kitaru caches checkpoints by input hash; `run_gates_step(project_dir)` called twice across retries would play back the FIRST attempt's report on the second attempt. Content-distinguishing args make false cache hits impossible | 3.3 section pipeline |
| 2026-07-27 | Manifest commits per attempt (gate 4 needs it on disk), then rolls back to the pre-attempt state when gates fail | Committing only after gates pass | Gate 4 validates source node-ids against the on-disk manifest, so gating needs the proposals applied; rolling back a failed attempt restores a service-produced state, preserving "proposals commit only at section.validated" (pipeline 5.3) with no garbage | 3.3 section pipeline |
| 2026-07-27 | index.tsx is written deterministically by the orchestrator in M3's single-section skeleton | LLM assembly call per pipeline 2.5 | With exactly one section, assembly is mechanical (import + render with nodeId); the page agent's own assembly step arrives with multi-section fan-out in M5 | 3.3 section pipeline |
| 2026-07-27 | The bounded retry is an in-flow loop with the failure report appended to the user prompt; each attempt is a fresh checkpoint | Kitaru replay-with-overrides per retry | Within a live run, a loop with modified input is semantically identical (changed input = changed output) and simpler; replay-with-overrides is reserved for its real job — M4 section regeneration forking recorded runs | 3.3 section pipeline |
| 2026-07-27 | hero.md 1.0.0 → 1.0.1: external URLs must use placeholder domains unless the brief supplies real ones; copy facts must stay internally consistent | Leaving 1.0.0 (thresholds were met) | Soak review evidence: soak-07 invented a plausible vimeo.com URL (fabricated third-party links are a handover hazard); soak-09 contradicted itself week-vs-month. Both are cheap prompt-side fixes with direct evidence (docs/reports/m3-soak.md) | 3.4 soak |
| 2026-07-27 | Regeneration forks via `flow.replay(exec_id, at="generate_section", flow_overrides={regen_block, regen_overridden_ids})` — the REGEN BLOCK is a flow parameter, not a checkpoint input override | checkpoint_overrides on generate_section's input | Flow-parameter overrides let the flow body recompute prompts naturally, so gate-failure retries inside a regen carry BOTH the regen context and the failure report; a checkpoint input override would pin every retry to the overridden prompt and lose the report. prepare_workspace sits before the anchor and plays back — the existing project is never reset | 4.1 regen |
| 2026-07-27 | Gate 7 also fails "false orphans" (declared in orphanedOverrides but still attached) | Only the contract-letter check (missing + undeclared) | A false orphan makes the editor tell the user their edit lost its target while the element still exists — the user discards a live edit. Same silent-drop hazard, opposite direction; M4.3's "orphan correctness" measures exactly this | 4.1 gate 7 |
| 2026-07-27 | `replaceSection` validates proposals against the manifest with the section's ACTIVE entries stripped (tombstoned entries retained) | A bespoke validation path | Surviving IDs must not trip the duplicate-active guard, while retained tombstones keep resurrection rules enforced across successive regenerations — one propose/commit implementation stays the single source of validation truth | 4.1 manifest |
| 2026-07-27 | Multi-process manifest commits are serialized via a portable file lock (`compiler/src/manifest-lock.ts`, exclusive-create + stale-lock steal); rollback for first-generation commits uses a NEW `rollback-commit` CLI command that deletes exactly this attempt's own node IDs (never a blind snapshot overwrite) | Trusting the existing unlocked read-modify-write; blind-overwrite rollback for all paths | Pipeline 2.5's "pages are parallel" means concurrent OS processes commit to the SAME manifest.json; an unlocked cycle silently loses a writer's proposals (proven: an 8-process race smoke test lost writes before the lock, none after), and a blind-overwrite rollback would erase a concurrent sibling's commit that landed between this attempt's commit and its own gate failure. Regeneration's blind-overwrite rollback is kept AS IS — it is always single-session, never concurrent | 5.3 fan-out |
| 2026-07-27 | Missing archetype templates (all but hero) get ONE shared `generic-section.md` fallback (structural guidance + archetype description from the catalog, no canonical example) until M5.4 adds dedicated templates; the siteplan still names the real archetype (e.g. "feature-grid"), so this never counts against the plan's budgeted `custom` escape hatch | Blocking fan-out until M5.4; mislabeling every section as `custom` | M5.3's job is proving the fan-out MECHANISM (checkpoints, parallelism, manifest concurrency, crash recovery) on real multi-section pages; gating it on five more tuned templates first would leave the mechanism unproven. `custom` stays reserved for the Planner's own explicit fallback (contract 4.4's 1-per-page budget), which this doesn't touch | 5.3 fan-out |
| 2026-07-27 | A route's N sections are generated as N separate `generate_section_flow` executions (looped by page_worker.py), not one monolithic multi-section flow | A single @flow with repeated checkpoint calls for all sections | Kitaru auto-suffixes repeated calls to the same checkpoint function (generate_section, generate_section_2, ...) with no stable per-section identity, making targeted resume-after-crash ambiguous. Each section as its own flow execution reuses the EXACT resume idiom already proven in 3.1/4.1 (`replay(exec_id, at=..., skip=[...])`) with zero new invention; page-level "resume" becomes "skip sections already recorded in progress JSON, don't touch the rest" | 5.3 fan-out |
| 2026-07-27 | routes.ts is written deterministically by the orchestrator from the site plan; the Shell Agent (LLM) authors only AppShell/Nav/Footer, importing routes read-only | Letting the Shell Agent also emit routes.ts | Contract section 2 calls routes.ts "the ground-truth route table"; if the model authored it, a hallucinated or dropped route becomes silently possible. Determinism here mirrors index.tsx/gallery-page precedent: mechanical correctness where correctness is mechanical, LLM only for the genuinely creative parts (Nav/Footer styling and copy) | 5.3 shell agent |
| 2026-07-27 | Gemini added as an OPT-IN provider escape hatch (`ORCH_MODEL_PROVIDER=gemini`, default remains `anthropic`) via a dispatch layer in `model_call.py` | Switching the project's target model; a separate parallel pipeline | The contract names the Claude API specifically (pipeline 5.1) — this is infrastructure flexibility to keep development moving during an Anthropic account billing outage, not a target-architecture change. Every prompt/doc still assumes Anthropic; default behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged when the env var is unset (verified: existing tier-resolution tests pass with no env var) | 5.3 fan-out (billing outage) |
| 2026-07-27 | The editor triggers regens through preview-server endpoints (`POST /__regen`, `/__regen-revert`); real mode spawns the orchestrator CLI, mock mode (WG_REGEN_MOCK=1) applies deterministic file transformations for CI-hermetic UX e2e | A separate orchestrator HTTP service; live-model e2e | The preview server already owns the project and its lifetime; CI has no API key, so the UX suite runs against the mock while the real engine is covered by 4.1's live checks and 4.3's stress suite | 4.2 regen UX |
| 2026-07-27 | Revert-regeneration restores a server-side pre-regen snapshot of the page dir + manifest (one step, PRD 4.4); the Kitaru recording chain is unaffected | Forking the recorded run back via checkpoint replay | File-level restore is equivalent in effect for one-step revert: the next regen builds its REGEN BLOCK from disk and overrides the fork's flow args, so the restored state dominates regardless of which recording anchors the fork. Presented as a header action beside Undo/Redo; deep history-line integration is M7 polish | 4.2 regen UX |
| 2026-07-27 | After regen/revert the editor force-reloads the frame, and the regen endpoints invalidate Vite's module graph before responding | Trusting HMR | Whole-section rewrites and directory-swap reverts are beyond HMR's reliable delivery, and the editor's immediate reload RACES the watcher's async invalidation — observed as a stale frame in e2e; explicit invalidate + reload is deterministic, and frame:ready re-applies overrides | 4.2 regen UX |
| 2026-07-27 | Rebase-style workspace resets pass a distinguishing `workspace_token` checkpoint arg | Relying on prepare_workspace re-running | Kitaru cache-hits play back a checkpoint's recorded OUTPUT without re-running its side effects — a cached prepare_workspace leaves the directory untouched. Any checkpoint whose side effect matters must carry input that changes when the effect is needed | 4.3 stress |
| 2026-07-27 | M5 pre-work flagged: gate 4 must learn map-derived node IDs (template-literal `nodeId` inside `.map()`) before list archetypes land | Leaving gate 4 literal-only | Stress case c10 showed the model following contract 5.2's own list-item pattern and failing gate 4 three times for it — feature-grid/pricing-tiers/social-proof will hit this constantly. The stress suite's only failed case; not an ID-survival defect | 4.3 stress (docs/reports/m4-id-survival.md) |
| 2026-07-27 | Intake's one-clarification-max is enforced STRUCTURALLY: the follow-up call's tool schema has no clarifyingQuestions field | Prompt instruction + orchestrator counting | A schema that cannot express a second round beats trusting the model or policing output; the orchestrator's round logic stays trivial | 5.1 intake |
| 2026-07-27 | Plans live in `<project>/plan/` ({brief,siteplan,plan-status}.json), served/edited/approved via preview-server endpoints; `prepare_workspace` preserves plan/ across resets; the generation CLI refuses to spend on an unapproved plan (plan-less projects exempt for skeleton/soak/stress runs) | A separate plan store outside the project | "Project state is the atomic unit" (PRD 6); the preview server already owns the project dir and its endpoints are the editor's established seam. The exemption keeps M3/M4 tooling working until M5 wires plans into generation | 5.1 plan approval |
| 2026-07-27 | Landing priors (4-7 sections, hero first, cta-band last) are HARD validator rules; other page archetypes get prompt-side priors only | Hard rules for every page archetype | Pipeline 4.3 states exact numbers only for landing; inventing hard bounds for the others would encode guesses as failures. The validator's messages feed the planner's bounded retry, same pattern as gates | 5.1 planner |
| 2026-07-27 | Primitive generation is ONE streamed model call against static per-primitive specs (PRIMITIVE_SPECS: fixed props shapes/variant unions/nodeId+className rules); validation = exact 15-file set + tsc + gates with bounded retry | One call per primitive; open generation | Pipeline 2.3 specifies two steps with one checkpoint each and calls primitive generation "constrained fill-in"; fixed props shapes keep every generated system drop-in compatible with page agents and the editor. Streaming required by the SDK's long-request guard at 24k max_tokens | 5.2 design system |
| 2026-07-27 | The DS agent emits `design-inventory.json` (primitive signature lines) into the workspace; `generate_section_flow(reuse_workspace=True)` skips the fixture reset and builds DESIGN CONTEXT from the workspace's tokens.json + inventory | Re-deriving signatures from generated source | Pipeline section 9 says the DS agent emits the inventory as structured output; parsing generated TSX to reconstruct signatures would be a second implementation that drifts. The fixture remains the compiler test bed (stub table) | 5.2 design system |
| 2026-07-27 | The primitive gallery page is DETERMINISTIC (built by the orchestrator, no LLM), carries no node ids, and replaces the blank page until sections generate | LLM-generated gallery | The gallery is eyeball-review infrastructure, not product output; determinism means a gallery failure implicates the primitives, never the gallery. No node ids keeps it outside manifest jurisdiction (gate 4) | 5.2 design system |

| 2026-07-28 | **Real, pre-existing data-loss bug found and fixed**: the debounced-persistence effect now skips its very first post-hydration run (a `skipNextSaveRef`, set alongside `hydratedRef.current = true` in bootstrap) | Leaving the effect to fire on every `history` change unconditionally, relying on `hydratedRef.current` alone to gate it | Bootstrap's own `setHistory(persisted)` is itself a `history` change — `hydratedRef.current` is set to `true` synchronously right after, in the same tick, so by the time React runs the persistence effect for THAT render, the guard is already satisfied, and the effect schedules a redundant "startup save" that writes the just-loaded data straight back. If a real edit's own save cycle starts while that startup save is still in flight (routine once the debounce is ~300ms and the multi-route persistence effect now does more work per cycle — several parallel per-route PUTs, not one), the two saves race, and whichever's fetches settle last wins on disk — sometimes the stale pre-edit one, silently dropping the edit. Reproduced directly: "Saving…"/"Saved" text observed correctly, override committed in React state, yet the on-disk overrides file was empty immediately after. Not new to this session's canvas work, but the wider per-cycle work made the race window big enough to surface reliably once the e2e suite grew large enough to run under heavier system load | 5.4 (flaky "edits persist across reload" across style/text/visibility specs, traced past several dead-end test-timing theories to a real application race) |
| 2026-07-28 | `helpers.waitForSaved` tolerates missing the transient "Saving…" state (falls back to a fixed wait past the known 300ms debounce before checking "Saved") instead of requiring it be observed | Requiring "Saving…" to be seen before checking "Saved" (the original implementation) | Secondary, genuine hardening alongside the persistence-race fix above: even with that race fixed, a fast debounce cycle completing between two Playwright polls is possible under load, and the original assertion would fail outright rather than just being slower to confirm. Kept even though the race fix addresses the specific data-loss bug this was first suspected to be — this only changes how patiently we CONFIRM a save, never what data gets saved | 5.4 (waitForSaved hardening, alongside the persistence-race investigation) |

| 2026-07-30 | **Image replace implemented as the TEXT channel carrying a `key`** (`{nodeId, channel:"text", key:"src", value}`), exactly as PRD 3.5 specifies ("override channel `text` with key `src` (content, not style)") | Adding an `image` channel | Following the PRD here was cheaper AND better: an image swap compiles through the SAME mock-data rewrite as any other content edit, so it needed no new compilation path, no new gate, no new override kind, and no change to gate 4/7. The key generalises the text channel rather than special-casing images: absent means the node's text child (every ordinary copy edit), present means the named JSX attribute. The one real difference is resolution -- an Image is self-closing and has no text child, so the exporter reads the field from the `src` ATTRIBUTE's bound expression instead | 7.7 |

Same call as 6.3's FailedSectionPlaceholder: the fixture is the permanent test bed, so the shapes the compiler must handle belong in it | 7.7 |
| 2026-07-30 | The invariant suite's frame-panning helper became TARGET-SEEKING (pan until the route's iframe mounts, dispatching the wheel at the stage element rather than a screen point), and its per-route readiness check now supports a DOM-attribute condition alongside the stylesheet-substring one | Keeping the fixed-delta pan and the stylesheet-only readiness check | Both broke the moment a second route joined the suite with a non-style override. The fixed pan was correct only for whichever case ran first -- adding the image case ahead of the contact-form case made the later pan overshoot, and overshooting puts the frame out of range on the far side just as surely as undershooting. Dispatching at the stage element also removes the hit-testing hazard documented in 6.2 (a fixed screen point can end up inside a live cross-origin iframe, which swallows the wheel silently). The readiness check assumed every route had a style override to wait on; `about` carries only the image case, and image replace IS the text channel, so it injects no stylesheet at all and the wait could never have fired | 7.7 |
