WAL was NOT done now because verifying it needs a paid run — recorded as K1, and it is what would let parallelism return | dogfood 2026-08-12 |
| 2026-08-14 | **G5: generated sites reference images as an inline SVG data URI, never a URL.** A new digest rule 9 in all 28 archetype templates tells the page agent to declare one module-level `PLACEHOLDER_IMAGE` const per mock data file and use it for every image `src`; the six templates whose canonical example had images now demonstrate it. `orchestrator/src/orchestrator/placeholder_image.py` holds the canonical URI plus a deterministic backstop that REWRITES an unloadable image URL at the single section write funnel (`write_files_repairing_images`, used by both write paths) and announces it on stdout | A real placeholder service (placehold.co / picsum / unsplash); local placeholder files committed under the project's `public/`; omitting images and reserving the layout space; failing the section (a retry with a failure report) instead of repairing it; putting the rule in `build_design_context` so it reaches every template from one place | **`.example` is reserved by IANA (RFC 2606) so that it can never resolve** — so every image in the dogfood site was broken on the tester's first view, and round 1 had already reported "ten broken `.example` images from two different invented hostnames" and nothing changed. Six templates' worked examples literally taught `images.yourbrand.example/....jpg`, and digest rule 6 tells the agent to prefer placeholder domains for external URLs, so the model was doing as instructed. **The handover constraint decided the form**: the export zip is the deliverable, so a placeholder service puts a third-party network dependency inside it (dead on a plane, behind a proxy, in a sealed CI container) and would make the invariant suite's preview-vs-export screenshot diff depend on a remote host's uptime and bytes. A `public/` file always resolves offline but the reference is a URL and the site is served from two different bases — root-absolute `/placeholder.svg` 404s under the hosted proxy's `/preview/<id>/`, the one surface a tester looks at — while an `import` needs a `*.svg` module declaration for gate 1's `tsc --noEmit` and turns a filename typo into a broken image no gate can see. A data URI has no host, no base, no import and no file that can go missing: identical at `/`, under the proxy, in `vite build`, in the zip, and offline, which also makes it deterministic for the screenshot diff. **REPAIR rather than refuse**, unlike the `<UNKNOWN>` brand-name backstop: there only a human knows the right value so refusing at the $0.003 intake call is cheapest, whereas here the correct value is a fixed constant, so a rewrite costs nothing, burns no retry, and never turns a grey swatch into a lost section (an exhausted section ships a `FailedSectionPlaceholder`, strictly worse for the tester). **Two encoding details are load-bearing, not cosmetic**: the fill's `#` is written `%23` because a literal `#e4e7ec` in mock data matches gate 3's `RAW_HEX`, which scans every in-scope file and would have failed EVERY section with an image on every retry; and the SVG uses single quotes so it drops into a double-quoted TS string unescaped. The rule went in the digest rather than `build_design_context` because that block carries a documented <600-token budget (pipeline 4.1) with only ~185 tokens of headroom, and silently eating it to save 27 edits is the wrong trade; a test parameterized over every template is what keeps a new archetype from missing the rule. Scope held to images: `docs-toc-page`'s `docs.yourbrand.example` **hrefs** are deliberate (its own rule 6) and `detail-drawer`'s `mediaSrc` PDF is a document, so the repair requires a reserved host AND image evidence, and a non-image extension is a positive signal to leave the value alone. **UNVERIFIED AGAINST A LIVE MODEL** — no paid generation was run, so the claim proven is that the instruction reaches every archetype's rendered prompt and that the backstop catches the exact value the live run emitted, not that a model obeys the prose | dogfood 2026-08-14, finding G5 |
| 2026-08-14 | **G7: the container `node_modules` volumes are NAMED and SHARED by both services, not anonymous and per-service.** Five declared volumes (`root_/compiler_/editor_/server_/fixture_node_modules`) replace five anonymous mounts on each of the two services; `compose.yaml` gains a top-level `volumes:` block and the editor gains a healthcheck that gates nothing, so `compose ps` and `up --wait` can answer "is it up yet?" | Keeping anonymous volumes and only documenting the slow start; per-service named volumes (10, isolated) rather than 5 shared; dropping the two empty mounts (`compiler/`, `editor/`); a tmpfs; baking `node_modules` in and dropping the `/app` bind mount; adding a wrapper script to print progress during the silent phase | **A tester spent ~50 minutes over 4 attempts and 2 engine restarts, ended in a silent hang, and accumulated 92 orphaned volumes / 3.39 GB — and one word, `anonymous`, caused all three symptoms.** Measured with the image already built: an anonymous volume is per-container and is NEVER reused across a `down`/`up`, so each `up` created **10** fresh volumes (5 per service) and copied **478 MB** of `node_modules` into them; `up -d` took **61.1s** then **27.1s**, against **13.4s** named (**12.2–12.9s** over 5 cycles; 22.2s from zero volumes, and that figure additionally waits for BOTH services healthy). That copy runs inside the phase compose prints as `Container … Creating` and then says nothing more about for its whole duration — **48.1s** for the second container, 12.8s on the next run, against 5.8s for the first — so a 48-second silence follows a single line, which is indistinguishable from a hang and was reported as one. And a plain `docker compose down`, **the command the README itself told testers to use**, detaches all ten without deleting them: **+10 orphans per cycle** (measured, dangling 25→35 across one `down`), unreachable by `down -v` afterwards because it only removes volumes still ATTACHED to the containers it removes, and unfilterable because an anonymous volume carries only `com.docker.volume.anonymous=` and no compose project label — so the sole command that reaches them is a blanket `docker volume prune`, which takes every other project's anonymous volumes with it. 92 orphans is ~9 cycles of exactly that. Named volumes fix all three at once and add a progress signal that was previously impossible: compose prints a `Volume … Created` line per volume and names each one it removes on `down -v`, and `--filter label=com.docker.compose.project=websitegenerator` enumerates them. **SHARED rather than per-service** because both services run the SAME image, need the identical linux-x64 tree, and already share every other file in `/app` through the bind mount — the per-container isolation was incidental to anonymity, never designed — which halves both the first-start copy (239 MB) and the disk; only the editor writes into any of them (Vite's dep cache) and only the editor runs that Vite, while the preview children the server spawns write into `generated/`, a bind mount. The two EMPTY mounts stay: `compiler/node_modules` and `editor/node_modules` hold 0 entries because npm workspaces hoists to the root, but their job is to shadow whatever the HOST has there, and a host install can legitimately place a win32 binary at that path when a version conflict defeats hoisting — an empty volume costs nothing. **Three plausible causes were RULED OUT by measurement rather than argument**, which is the part worth keeping: `stop_grace_period: 30s` never engages (`down` is 1.9–4.3s, both containers stop in under 2s); the healthcheck passes 5.5s after `Starting` on all six starts, and the realistic boot failure — a missing master key — is already loud and fast (**4.8s** to the entrypoint's full message plus `dependency failed to start: … exited (1)`, non-zero exit); and disk pressure is not a factor at all, the VM reporting **925 GB** free of 1007 GB — the "~3.8 GB" in the finding is the VM's **RAM**, which the README's own RAM row states. WAL SQLite on the bind mount showed no symptom either: the server booted, resolved a real `code version`, and a login POST returned the uniform 401, which means a real argon2id verification ran, so the native binary these volumes exist to protect loads. **NOT established**: why the second container's five volumes cost 2–8× the first's (writeback contention is a guess; sharing removes the second copy rather than explaining it), and no 7.5-minute instance was reproduced — the phase and its silence were, at 48.1s. Verified by running: 6 starts / 6 downs including a clean-slate first start and 5 plain-`down` cycles, **0 new orphans** in every measurement, `down -v` removing exactly the five named volumes and leaving an unrelated project's untouched, and `npm run check` at real exit 0. The 35 orphans already on the machine are stranded by construction and need one operator command, which the README now documents | dogfood 2026-08-14, finding G7 |
| 2026-08-14 | A failed `generate` job's `error` is BUILT from the orchestrator's own structured report, not tailed around it (`server/src/orchestrator-failure.ts`): find `{"failed_stage": ...}` anywhere in stdout by brace-matching a key anchor, render it ABOVE both raw stream tails, unescape each leaf ONCE, and clip PER-LEAF keeping the informative end with an announced character count. Also extends `redact.ts`'s `AQ.` pattern to match after an escape sequence, not only after a word boundary | (a) keep the previous fix's blind 1500-char tail of each stream; (b) add a dedicated sentinel line to `acceptance.py` and split on it; (c) truncate fan-out's `detail` structurally on the Python side, since `json.dumps(detail)[:6000]` can cut mid-JSON; (d) parse the report and drop the raw output | A dogfood generation failed after 673s having spent $1.2767 and its whole recorded reason was 1592 characters of nested-escaped, THRICE-truncated Python `repr` of a Kitaru database row: `.slice(-1500)` opened its window AFTER the report's own `{`, so the row held the report's tail with nothing to say it was one. (b) is unnecessary -- `"failed_stage"` already IS a sentinel and that print is shared with `acceptance_edit.py`; (c) is an orchestrator change whose only honest verification is a live billed run, and the TypeScript side now tolerates an unparseable `detail` anyway; (d) is refused outright, because the failure whose shape is NOT the expected report (a traceback, an OOM kill, a missing interpreter) is exactly when raw bytes matter most -- so both tails always ship, at the full 1500 each when no report parsed. The `redact.ts` extension is separate and predates this fix in every caller (`preview-pool.ts` logs a child's stdout the same way): a boundary-anchored pattern cannot see `...\nAQ.<key>`, and an escaped newline before a key is the exact shape this artefact is full of. Verified against the real rows rather than a fixture -- three real failures are checked in as `server/src/fixtures/real-generate-failures.json`, machine-generated from `job.error` in the gitignored identity database -- and by 18 perturbations, 16 red; the 3 that stayed green are named in the G1 report rather than smoothed over, along with the two tests of my own they proved inert | dogfood 2026-08-14, finding G1 |
| 2026-08-12 | **G4: text-edit discoverability is taught as a GESTURE in three places, and the inspector deliberately gains NO text field** — a hover chip beside the node label (only when that node's `editable` contains `text`), a callout at the TOP of the inspector above every control, and the channel pills restyled as a status (`cursor: default`, no fill) with the gesture written beside them | Adding a text input to the inspector; a tooltip only; documenting the gesture in the README | A field is the obvious fix and is **actively harmful here**: the parent frame cannot know a node's text, because the shim sends it only on `dblclick` — so the field would open EMPTY and a blur would commit an empty override that **wipes the copy**. The callout sits at the top because the measured failure was a tester scrolling to the bottom of the inspector and concluding the channel did not exist; the dogfood operator initially concluded text editing was **not implemented** and found it only by reading source. A content cursor was considered and is unreachable: it belongs to the shim inside a cross-origin frame, and `compiler/` was out of that task's scope | dogfood G4 |
| 2026-08-12 | **G3: a generation that lost a manifest entry now reports a distinct `blocked` export state** — keyed on gate 4's machine-readable reasons, carrying **no retry button at all**, and reachability-aware: it names "Regenerate whole page" when the page is on the canvas and "generate again" when the route is missing from the canvas entirely. Every other export failure keeps its honest retry | A generic "export failed" with a retry; blocking the export button outright | Gate 4 rejects a section file with no manifest entry **permanently** — the loss happened upstream, so no retry can succeed. Offering one is the same class of lie as the placeholder advice fixed earlier on this branch (`07c23e2`): it sends a tester to do something impossible. Reachability-awareness matters because the two cases have different remedies and the canvas cannot tell them apart from the export alone. **Recorded as still open**: the canvas cannot warn BEFORE an export that a route is missing, because its only route source is the very file the entry was lost from | dogfood G3 |
| 2026-08-12 | **G6: the free self-healing first-run failure is recognised by Kitaru's own stale-stack marker**, framed as expected, states **$0.00 charged** (gated on the failure being at the first stage), makes "Submit the brief again" the primary action, and hides the raw `uv`/Kitaru noise behind a disclosure | Suppressing the failure; leaving "Resume" primary; recognising it by exit code | A fresh container's first generation ALWAYS fails at ~13s having spent $0.00 — gitignored `orchestrator/.kitaru/` host state reaches a container whose Kitaru DB was created fresh, so Kitaru refuses rather than running on a different stack, then rewrites the config and self-heals. Presented as 30 lines of raw subprocess output it reads as a hard crash, and the primary CTA was **"Resume"** while the documented fix is **submit again**. The $0.00 claim is gated rather than unconditional because a later-stage failure genuinely has spent money and must not be told otherwise. Matching one dependency's message text is a known fragility: a miss degrades to today's screen rather than to a wrong claim | dogfood G6 |
| 2026-08-14 | **C1 (the whole-branch review's Critical): digest rule 9 now INLINES the placeholder data URI at every image field, and explicitly forbids hoisting it into a shared `PLACEHOLDER_IMAGE` const.** All 28 archetype templates rewritten (frontmatter versions bumped), the six worked examples with images now show a quoted literal per field, and `placeholder_image.PLACEHOLDER_CONST_NAME` is renamed `HOISTED_CONST_NAME` because the name now appears in the prompt only as the anti-pattern it names | Teaching the exporter to resolve a const-bound leaf (rejected: the contract wins all conflicts, and one const feeds many items, so a rewrite through it would silently change every image sharing it); leaving rule 9 alone and accepting that image replace is broken | `docs/codegen-contract-v1.md` §7.1 defines the text channel as rewriting the mock data **literal**, and `exporter.ts` implements exactly that in two places, each resolving the leaf via `.asKind(SyntaxKind.StringLiteral)` and throwing otherwise. Image replace (PRD 3.5 / feature 7.7) IS that channel with key `src` — so a hoisted const turned one image edit into a PERMANENT whole-export failure, naming a mock field the user never saw, with no gate report, which made the editor classify it **retryable** and offer a button that can never work: the exact defect G3 was written to remove, re-created by two reasonable changes meeting. Verbosity in generated mock data is disposable output; a broken export is not. **The prompt half cannot be verified without a live generation and was NOT run** (~$1.45) — what is proven is deterministic: the rule reaches all 28 rendered prompts, no example teaches the forbidden shape, and the exporter's refusal is now pinned by a test that exports a project shaped the way generation shapes them | whole-branch review of `fix/dogfood-findings`, C1 |
| 2026-08-14 | **C1's second half: `inline_hoisted_string_consts` — a deterministic backstop that rewrites `const X = "lit"; … field: X` back into `field: "lit"`, in mock data files only.** Wired into `write_files_repairing_images`, announced on stdout like the image repair, and generalised past images to ANY hoisted string const | Prompt-only (rejected: sharing a long repeated literal is what a competent programmer does everywhere else, so this is the likeliest disobedience, and a Critical should not rest on prose); failing the section instead of repairing it (rejected for the same reason G5 chose repair — an exhausted section ships as a `FailedSectionPlaceholder`, strictly worse than verbose mock data); a special case for the placeholder URI only | Deterministic before generative: a prompt change cannot be tested by running it, and this half can be. Narrow by construction — column-0 declarations only (module scope), non-`export`ed only (so removing the declaration cannot break the section file's import beside it), double-quoted escape-free values only, and identifier hits inside strings/comments are skipped because `_TS_ATOM` consumes those first. Generalised rather than image-specific because the defect class is wider: a shared `const CTA_LABEL` breaks a plain copy edit identically, and the general rule is simpler than the special case. A reference inside `${…}` is deliberately NOT inlined (no worse than today) and an unreferenced const is left where the model put it | whole-branch review of `fix/dogfood-findings`, C1 |
| 2026-08-14 | **I1: a page worker's deterministic-repair warning is lifted out of its subprocess.** `fanout.repair_warnings` scans each worker's FULL stdout for `REPAIR_WARNING_PREFIX` (a constant both sides import), the parent re-prints every hit, and the aggregate rides `run_fanout`'s result **outside `workers`**; `acceptance.py` forwards it in the run summary | Emitting a `run_event` per repair (more machinery, and the run report is not what a tester reads); surfacing `stdout_tail` for successful workers too (the tail is 1500 chars of Kitaru checkpoint chatter — the same truncation this branch spent G1 proving destroys a signal) | The repair's author called the stdout warning the ONLY signal that rule 9 has stopped working, but every section write happens inside a `page_worker` subprocess: `stdout_tail` has exactly one occurrence in the repo (its own assignment), `fanout.main()` strips `workers` before printing, and `acceptance.py` keeps only FAILED workers' `stderr_tail`. So on a SUCCESSFUL run — the only run where the repair is the sole evidence — the warning reached no log, no run report, no `job.error` and no UI. Outside `workers` deliberately, because anything nested there is stripped. Regeneration and add-section always surfaced it; they run in the process whose stdout the job captures | whole-branch review of `fix/dogfood-findings`, I1 |
| 2026-08-14 | **I2: the README's two halves are reconciled on `WEBGEN_FANOUT_MAX_WORKERS`, and the wall-clock figures' provenance is corrected rather than re-measured or rounded.** The from-source section no longer claims unset means "one worker per route at once"; both the $/time headline and the pre-Generate warning now state that the nine-to-eleven-minute range was measured with fan-out running TWO workers in parallel and is a floor, not a prediction; "a parallel page fan-out" in the architecture list is corrected. A new test reads the README and fails if any mention of the variable is not near the word "serial", or if either false phrasing returns | Re-measuring under serial fan-out (costs a paid generation); deleting the timings (they are the only measured figures there are); leaving the from-source half alone | Two agents last touched the two halves: G7 fixed the Docker half and the RAM row, and the from-source half kept asserting the opposite default AND an untrue provenance for the timings — a contributor reading that path got the wrong model of the default and a false basis for the numbers. Cost is unaffected by serialisation (it changes when calls happen, not how many), so only the time claim needed qualifying, and it is qualified honestly: no re-measurement has been run, and that is stated in the README itself. Nothing in the suite read the README, which is why the contradiction could not fail a test; the new test is deliberately narrow — a claim about the unset default, whose truth `max_parallel_workers()` decides — rather than a prose linter | whole-branch review of `fix/dogfood-findings`, I2 |

| 2026-08-14 | `gates.test.ts`'s fixture copy skips only the fixture's OWN top-level `dist/` (`relative(cleanFixture, src).split(sep)[0] !== "dist"`) instead of `!src.includes("dist")` | Leaving it; excluding `node_modules` from the copy instead | The substring form matched the whole absolute path, so it also stripped `node_modules/react-router-dom/dist/` — the package's type declarations. The copied project then failed `tsc` on `src/main.tsx` (missing module) BEFORE reaching the defect the test deliberately introduces, so `failures[0]` named the wrong file and the assertion broke. **It passes on the development machine for an accidental reason**: a `node_modules` exists in an ANCESTOR of `%TEMP%`, so Node's resolver finds the types anyway. Inside the shipped Docker image — and on `ubuntu-latest`, which `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs `npm run check` on — there is no such ancestor, the gate exits 1, and **CI is red while the host stays green**. Measured in the container, not inferred. **The perturbation deliberately does NOT fail here and that is itself the finding**: restoring the substring filter still passes 44/44 on this host, so this machine cannot discriminate and only CI or the container can. Excluding `node_modules` wholesale was rejected — the fixture's own `node_modules` is exactly why these tests "borrow the fixture wholesale", since gate 1 runs a real `tsc` | verification run 2026-08-14 |
